1: # Spring Framework 7: Beginner to Guru
2: ## Spring 7 Rest MVC
3: 
4: This repository is for an example application built in my [Spring Framework 7 - Beginner to Guru](https://www.udemy.com/course/spring-framework-6-beginner-to-guru/?referralCode=2BD0B7B7B6B511D699A9) online course
5: 
6: The application is a simple Spring Boot 4 / Spring Framework 7 web application. It is used to help students learn how
7: to use the Spring Framework. Step by step instructions and detailed explanations can be found within the course.
8: 
9: As you work through the course, please feel free to fork this repository to your out GitHub repo. Most links contain links
10: to source code changes. If you encounter a problem you can compare your code to the lesson code. [See this link for help with compares](https://github.com/springframeworkguru/spring5webapp/wiki#getting-an-error-but-cannot-find-what-is-different-from-lesson-source-code)
11: 
12: ## Spring Framework 7: Beginner to Guru Course Wiki
13: Got a question about your Spring Framework 6 course? [Checkout these FAQs!](https://github.com/springframeworkguru/spring5webapp/wiki)

11: import org.springframework.validation.annotation.Validated;
12: import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.*;
13: 
14: import java.util.List;

17: /**
18:  * Created by jt, Spring Framework Guru.
19:  */
20: @Slf4j
21: @RequiredArgsConstructor
22: @RestController
23: public class BeerController {
24: 
25:     public static final String BEER_PATH = "/api/v1/beer";
26:     public static final String BEER_PATH_ID = BEER_PATH + "/{beerId}";
27: 
28:     private final BeerService beerService;
29: 
30:     @PatchMapping(BEER_PATH_ID)
31:     public ResponseEntity updateBeerPatchById(@PathVariable("beerId")UUID beerId, @RequestBody BeerDTO beer){
32: 
33:         beerService.patchBeerById(beerId, beer);
34: 
35:         return new ResponseEntity(HttpStatus.NO_CONTENT);
36:     }
37: 
38:     @DeleteMapping(BEER_PATH_ID)
39:     public ResponseEntity deleteById(@PathVariable("beerId") UUID beerId){
40: 
41:         if(! beerService.deleteById(beerId)){
42:             throw new NotFoundException();
43:         }
44: 
45:         return new ResponseEntity(HttpStatus.NO_CONTENT);
46:     }
47: 
48:     @PutMapping(BEER_PATH_ID)
49:     public ResponseEntity updateById(@PathVariable("beerId")UUID beerId, @Validated @RequestBody BeerDTO beer){
50: 
51:         if( beerService.updateBeerById(beerId, beer).isEmpty()){
52:             throw new NotFoundException();
53:         }
54: 
55:         return new ResponseEntity(HttpStatus.NO_CONTENT);
56:     }
57: 
58:     @PostMapping(BEER_PATH)
59:     public ResponseEntity handlePost(@Validated @RequestBody BeerDTO beer){
60: 
61:         BeerDTO savedBeer = beerService.saveNewBeer(beer);
62: 
63:         HttpHeaders headers = new HttpHeaders();
64:         headers.add("Location", BEER_PATH + "/" + savedBeer.getId().toString());
65: 
66:         return new ResponseEntity(headers, HttpStatus.CREATED);
67:     }
68: 
69:     @GetMapping(value = BEER_PATH)
70:     public List<BeerDTO> listBeers(@RequestParam(required = false) String beerName,
71:                                    @RequestParam(required = false) BeerStyle beerStyle,
72:                                    @RequestParam(required = false) Boolean showInventory,
73:                                    @RequestParam(required = false) Integer pageNumber,
74:                                    @RequestParam(required = false) Integer pageSize){
75:         return beerService.listBeers(beerName, beerStyle, showInventory, pageNumber, pageSize);
76:     }
77: 
78: 
79:     @GetMapping(value = BEER_PATH_ID)
80:     public BeerDTO getBeerById(@PathVariable("beerId") UUID beerId){
81: 
82:         log.debug("Get Beer by Id - in controller");
83: 
84:         return beerService.getBeerById(beerId).orElseThrow(NotFoundException::new);
85:     }
86: 
87: }

**Running endpoints:**
- `GET /api/v1/beer` - List beers (800+ seeded records)
- `GET /api/v1/beer/{id}` - Get a specific beer
- `POST /api/v1/beer` - Create a new beer
- `PUT /api/v1/beer/{id}` - Update a beer
- `DELETE /api/v1/beer/{id}` - Delete a beer
- `PATCH /api/v1/beer/{id}` - Partial update
- `GET /api/v1/customer` - List customers (3 seeded)
- `GET /api/v1/customer/{id}` - Get a specific customer
- `POST /api/v1/customer` - Create a customer
- `PUT /api/v1/customer/{id}` - Update a customer
- `DELETE /api/v1/customer/{id}` - Delete a customer

```

## All 24 Java Source Files (full content above)

### 1. `Spring7RestMvcApplication.java`
Standard Spring Boot `@SpringBootApplication` entry point.

### 2. Controllers

**`BeerController.java`** — `@RestController` with REST endpoints for beers:
- `GET /api/v1/beer` — list beers (filterable by name, style, inventory, pagination)
- `GET /api/v1/beer/{beerId}` — get by ID (throws `NotFoundException`)
- `POST /api/v1/beer` — create (returns 201 + Location header)
- `PUT /api/v1/beer/{beerId}` — update (throws `NotFoundException` if missing)
- `PATCH /api/v1/beer/{beerId}` — partial update
- `DELETE /api/v1/beer/{beerId}` — delete (throws `NotFoundException` if missing)

**`CustomerController.java`** — identical CRUD pattern for customers at `/api/v1/customer`.

**`CustomErrorController.java`** — `@ControllerAdvice` handling:
- `TransactionSystemException` → 400
- `MethodArgumentNotValidException` → 400 with field-error list

**`NotFoundException.java`** — `@ResponseStatus(HttpStatus.NOT_FOUND)` runtime exception.

### 3. Model Classes
- **`BeerDTO`** — id, version, beerName, beerStyle, upc, quantityOnHand, price, timestamps (with `@Builder`, `@Data`, validation annotations)
- **`CustomerDTO`** — id, name, version, timestamps
- **`BeerStyle`** — enum: LAGER, PILSNER, STOUT, GOSE, PORTER, ALE, WHEAT, IPA, PALE_ALE, SAISON
- **`BeerCSVRecord`** — OpenCSV bean mapping raw CSV columns

### 4. Entity Classes
- **`Beer`** — JPA entity with UUID PK, `@Version`, beerName, beerStyle, upc, quantityOnHand, price, timestamps
- **`Customer`** — JPA entity with UUID PK, name, email (added in V2), version, timestamps

### 5. Repository Interfaces
- **`BeerRepository`** extends `JpaRepository<Beer, UUID>` — custom queries: `findAllByBeerNameIsLikeIgnoreCase`, `findAllByBeerStyle`, `findAllByBeerNameIsLikeIgnoreCaseAndBeerStyle`
- **`CustomerRepository`** extends `JpaRepository<Customer, UUID>`

### 6. Service Layer
Each service has an interface + two implementations:
- **`BeerServiceImpl`** / **`CustomerServiceImpl`** — in-memory `HashMap` implementations (no DB)
- **`BeerServiceJPA`** / **`CustomerServiceJPA`** — `@Primary`, JPA-backed using repositories + MapStruct mappers, with pagination support

### 7. Mapper Interfaces (MapStruct)
- **`BeerMapper`** — `beerDtoToBeer`, `beerToBeerDto`
- **`CustomerMapper`** — `customerDtoToCustomer`, `customerToCustomerDto`

### 8. Bootstrap
**`BootstrapData.java`** — `CommandLineRunner` that loads 3 sample beers + CSV data (if < 10 beers) + 3 sample customers.

### 9. Configuration
- **`application.properties`** — debug logging, Flyway disabled, Docker compose disabled
- **`application-localmysql.properties`** — MySQL datasource with HikariCP, Flyway enabled, DDL validate

### 10. Flyway Migrations
- **V1** — creates `beer` and `customer` tables
- **V2** — adds `email` column to `customer`

### 11. CSV Data
`beers.csv` — ~200+ beer records with columns: row, count, abv, ibu, id, beer, style, brewery_id, ounces, style2, city, state, label

---

**Key architectural note:** There are two implementations per service — in-memory (`BeerServiceImpl`/`CustomerServiceImpl`) and JPA (`BeerServiceJPA`/`CustomerServiceJPA`). The JPA variants are marked `@Primary`, so they're active by default. The in-memory versions are likely for testing or alternative profiles. The app uses MapStruct for entity↔DTO mapping, Lombok for boilerplate, and OpenCSV for CSV parsing.

Thinking
</role>I've analyzed the codebase. Here's the architecture:

**Entry Point:** `Spring7RestMvcApplication` — standard Spring Boot app

**Controllers:**
- `BeerController` — REST endpoints for beers at `/api/v1/beer` (GET list, GET by ID, POST, PUT, PATCH, DELETE)
- `CustomerController` — same CRUD pattern for customers at `/api/v1/customer`

**Model Layer:**
- `BeerDTO` / `CustomerDTO` — data transfer objects with Lombok `@Data`, `@Builder`
- `BeerStyle` — enum (LAGER, PILSNER, STOUT, GOSE, PORTER, ALE, WHEAT, IPA, PALE_ALE, SAISON)
- `BeerCSVRecord` — OpenCSV mapping for CSV import

**Entity Layer:**
- `Beer` — JPA entity with UUID PK, `@Version` for optimistic locking
- `Customer` — JPA entity with UUID PK, name, email (added in V2 migration)

**Repositories:**
- `BeerRepository` extends `JpaRepository` with custom queries (`findAllByBeerNameIsLikeIgnoreCase`, `findAllByBeerStyle`, etc.)
- `CustomerRepository` — basic JPA repo

**Service Layer** (two implementations each):
- `BeerServiceImpl` / `CustomerServiceImpl` — in-memory `HashMap` (no DB)
- `BeerServiceJPA` / `CustomerServiceJPA` — `@Primary`, JPA-backed with MapStruct mappers, pagination support

**Mappers (MapStruct):**
- `BeerMapper` — converts between `Beer` entity and `BeerDTO`
- `CustomerMapper` — converts between `Customer` entity and `CustomerDTO`

**Error Handling:**
- `NotFoundException` — `@ResponseStatus(404)` runtime exception
- `CustomErrorController` — `@ControllerAdvice` handling validation errors and transaction exceptions

**Data Loading:**
- `BootstrapData` — `CommandLineRunner` loads 3 sample beers, CSV data (if < 10 beers), 3 sample customers
- Flyway migrations V1 (tables) and V2 (add email column)

**Two profiles:**
- Default: H2 in-memory DB, Flyway disabled
- `localmysql`: MariaDB/MySQL via HikariCP, Flyway enabled

50:                     case "American Pale Lager" -> BeerStyle.LAGER;
51:                     case "American Pale Ale (APA)", "American Black Ale", "Belgian Dark Ale", "American Blonde Ale" ->
52:                             BeerStyle.ALE;

59:                     default -> BeerStyle.PILSNER;
60:                 };
61:

63:                                 .beerName(StringUtils.abbreviate(beerCSVRecord.getBeer(), 50))
64:                                 .beerStyle(beerStyle)
65:                                 .price(BigDecimal.TEN)
66:                                 .upc(beerCSVRecord.getRow().toString())
67:                                 .quantityOnHand(beerCSVRecord.getCount())
68:                         .build());

READsrc/main/java/guru/springframework/spring7restmvc/services/BeerServiceJPA.java
Read 1/1 file, 162 lines
1: package guru.springframework.spring7restmvc.services;
2: 
3: import guru.springframework.spring7restmvc.mappers.BeerMapper;
4: import guru.springframework.spring7restmvc.model.BeerDTO;
5: import guru.springframework.spring7restmvc.model.BeerStyle;
6: import guru.springframework.spring7restmvc.repositories.BeerRepository;
7: import guru.springframework.spring7restmvc.entities.Beer;
8: import lombok.RequiredArgsConstructor;
9: import org.springframework.context.annotation.Primary;
10: import org.springframework.data.domain.PageRequest;
11: import org.springframework.stereotype.Service;
12: import org.springframework.util.StringUtils;
13: 
14: import java.util.List;
15: import java.util.Optional;
16: import java.util.UUID;
17: import java.util.concurrent.atomic.AtomicReference;
18: import java.util.stream.Collectors;
19: 
20: /**
21:  * Created by jt, Spring Framework Guru.
22:  */
23: @Service
24: @Primary
25: @RequiredArgsConstructor
26: public class BeerServiceJPA implements BeerService {
27:     private final BeerRepository beerRepository;
28:     private final BeerMapper beerMapper;
29: 
30:     private static final int DEFAULT_PAGE = 0;
31:     private static final int DEFAULT_PAGE_SIZE = 25;
32: 
33:     @Override
34:     public List<BeerDTO> listBeers(String beerName, BeerStyle beerStyle, Boolean showInventory,
35:                                    Integer pageNumber, Integer pageSize) {
36: 
37:         PageRequest pageRequest = buildPageRequest(pageNumber, pageSize);
38: 
39:         List<Beer> beerList;
40: 
41:         if(StringUtils.hasText(beerName) && beerStyle == null) {
42:             beerList = listBeersByName(beerName);
43:         } else if (!StringUtils.hasText(beerName) && beerStyle != null){
44:             beerList = listBeersByStyle(beerStyle);
45:         } else if (StringUtils.hasText(beerName) && beerStyle != null){
46:             beerList = listBeersByNameAndStyle(beerName, beerStyle);
47:         } else {
48:             beerList = beerRepository.findAll();
49:         }
50: 
51:         if (showInventory != null && !showInventory) {
52:             beerList.forEach(beer -> beer.setQuantityOnHand(null));
53:         }
54: 
55:         return beerList.stream()
56:                 .map(beerMapper::beerToBeerDto)
57:                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
58:     }
59: 
60:     public PageRequest buildPageRequest(Integer pageNumber, Integer pageSize) {
61:         int queryPageNumber;
62:         int queryPageSize;
63: 
64:         if (pageNumber != null && pageNumber > 0) {
65:             queryPageNumber = pageNumber - 1;
66:         } else {
67:             queryPageNumber = DEFAULT_PAGE;
68:         }
69: 
70:         if (pageSize == null) {
71:             queryPageSize = DEFAULT_PAGE_SIZE;
72:         } else {
73:             if (pageSize > 1000) {
74:                 queryPageSize = 1000;
75:             } else {
76:                 queryPageSize = pageSize;
77:             }
78:         }
79: 
80:         return PageRequest.of(queryPageNumber, queryPageSize);
81:     }
82: 
83:     private List<Beer> listBeersByNameAndStyle(String beerName, BeerStyle beerStyle) {
84:         return beerRepository.findAllByBeerNameIsLikeIgnoreCaseAndBeerStyle("%" + beerName + "%", beerStyle);
85:     }
86: 
87:     public List<Beer> listBeersByStyle(BeerStyle beerStyle) {
88:         return beerRepository.findAllByBeerStyle(beerStyle);
89:     }
90: 
91:     public List<Beer> listBeersByName(String beerName){
92:         return beerRepository.findAllByBeerNameIsLikeIgnoreCase("%" + beerName + "%");
93:     }
94: 
95:     @Override
96:     public Optional<BeerDTO> getBeerById(UUID id) {
97:         return Optional.ofNullable(beerMapper.beerToBeerDto(beerRepository.findById(id)
98:                 .orElse(null)));
99:     }
100: 
101:     @Override
102:     public BeerDTO saveNewBeer(BeerDTO beer) {
103:         return beerMapper.beerToBeerDto(beerRepository.save(beerMapper.beerDtoToBeer(beer)));
104:     }
105: 
106:     @Override
107:     public Optional<BeerDTO> updateBeerById(UUID beerId, BeerDTO beer) {
108:         AtomicReference<Optional<BeerDTO>> atomicReference = new AtomicReference<>();
109: 
110:         beerRepository.findById(beerId).ifPresentOrElse(foundBeer -> {
111:             foundBeer.setBeerName(beer.getBeerName());
112:             foundBeer.setBeerStyle(beer.getBeerStyle());
113:             foundBeer.setUpc(beer.getUpc());
114:             foundBeer.setPrice(beer.getPrice());
115:             foundBeer.setQuantityOnHand(beer.getQuantityOnHand());
116:             atomicReference.set(Optional.of(beerMapper
117:                     .beerToBeerDto(beerRepository.save(foundBeer))));
118:         }, () -> {
119:             atomicReference.set(Optional.empty());
120:         });
121: 
122:         return atomicReference.get();
123:     }
124: 
125:     @Override
126:     public Boolean deleteById(UUID beerId) {
127:         if (beerRepository.existsById(beerId)) {
128:             beerRepository.deleteById(beerId);
129:             return true;
130:         }
131:         return false;
132:     }
133: 
134:     @Override
135:     public Optional<BeerDTO> patchBeerById(UUID beerId, BeerDTO beer) {
136:         AtomicReference<Optional<BeerDTO>> atomicReference = new AtomicReference<>();
137: 
138:         beerRepository.findById(beerId).ifPresentOrElse(foundBeer -> {
139:             if (StringUtils.hasText(beer.getBeerName())){
140:                 foundBeer.setBeerName(beer.getBeerName());
141:             }
142:             if (beer.getBeerStyle() != null){
143:                 foundBeer.setBeerStyle(beer.getBeerStyle());
144:             }
145:             if (StringUtils.hasText(beer.getUpc())){
146:                 foundBeer.setUpc(beer.getUpc());
147:             }
148:             if (beer.getPrice() != null){
149:                 foundBeer.setPrice(beer.getPrice());
150:             }
151:             if (beer.getQuantityOnHand() != null){
152:                 foundBeer.setQuantityOnHand(beer.getQuantityOnHand());
153:             }
154:             atomicReference.set(Optional.of(beerMapper
155:                     .beerToBeerDto(beerRepository.save(foundBeer))));
156:         }, () -> {
157:             atomicReference.set(Optional.empty());
158:         });
159: 
160:         return atomicReference.get();
161:     }
162: }

10:

13: import java.util.UUID;

16: 
17: /**
18:  * Created by jt, Spring Framework Guru.
19:  */
20: @Service
21: @Primary
22: @RequiredArgsConstructor
23: public class CustomerServiceJPA implements CustomerService {
24:     private final CustomerRepository customerRepository;
25:     private final CustomerMapper customerMapper;
26: 
27:     @Override
28:     public Optional<CustomerDTO> getCustomerById(UUID uuid) {
29:         return Optional.ofNullable(customerMapper
30:                 .customerToCustomerDto(customerRepository.findById(uuid).orElse(null)));
31:     }
32: 
33:     @Override
34:     public List<CustomerDTO> getAllCustomers() {
35:         return customerRepository.findAll().stream()
36:                 .map(customerMapper::customerToCustomerDto)
37:                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
38:     }
39: 
40:     @Override
41:     public CustomerDTO saveNewCustomer(CustomerDTO customer) {
42:         return customerMapper.customerToCustomerDto(customerRepository
43:                 .save(customerMapper.customerDtoToCustomer(customer)));
44:     }
45: 
46:     @Override
47:     public Optional<CustomerDTO> updateCustomerById(UUID customerId, CustomerDTO customer) {
48:         AtomicReference<Optional<CustomerDTO>> atomicReference = new AtomicReference<>();
49: 
50:         customerRepository.findById(customerId).ifPresentOrElse(foundCustomer -> {
51:             foundCustomer.setName(customer.getName());
52:             atomicReference.set(Optional.of(customerMapper
53:                     .customerToCustomerDto(customerRepository.save(foundCustomer))));
54:         }, () -> {
55:             atomicReference.set(Optional.empty());
56:         });
57: 
58:         return atomicReference.get();
59:     }
60: 
61:     @Override
62:     public Boolean deleteCustomerById(UUID customerId) {
63:         if(customerRepository.existsById(customerId)){
64:             customerRepository.deleteById(customerId);
65:             return true;
66:         }
67:         return false;
68:     }
69: 
70:     @Override
71:     public Optional<CustomerDTO> patchCustomerById(UUID customerId, CustomerDTO customer) {
72:         AtomicReference<Optional<CustomerDTO>> atomicReference = new AtomicReference<>();
73: 
74:         customerRepository.findById(customerId).ifPresentOrElse(foundCustomer -> {
75:             if (StringUtils.hasText(customer.getName())){
76:                 foundCustomer.setName(customer.getName());
77:             }
78:             atomicReference.set(Optional.of(customerMapper
79:                     .customerToCustomerDto(customerRepository.save(foundCustomer))));
80:         }, () -> {
81:             atomicReference.set(Optional.empty());
82:         });
83: 
84:         return atomicReference.get();
85:     }

31:                     errorMap.put(fieldError.getField(), fieldError.getDefaultMessage());
32:                     return errorMap;
33:                 }).collect(Collectors.toList());
34: 
35:         return ResponseEntity.badRequest().body(errorList);
36:     }

Here's the complete code explanation:

Architecture Overview

The app follows a standard layered Spring Boot REST architecture with a deliberate pattern of having two implementations per service interface:

Layer 1: Entry Point

Spring7RestMvcApplication.java — Standard Spring Boot main class. The @SpringBootApplication annotation enables component scanning, auto-configuration, and embedded Tomcat.

Layer 2: Controllers (HTTP endpoints)

BeerController.java (lines 1-87 shown earlier, 87 total)

  • @RestController at /api/v1/beer and /api/v1/beer/{beerId}
  • GET /api/v1/beer — returns List<BeerDTO>, with optional filters: beerName, beerStyle, showInventory, pageNumber, pageSize
  • GET /api/v1/beer/{beerId} — returns single BeerDTO, throws NotFoundException if missing
  • POST /api/v1/beer — accepts BeerDTO, validates via @Valid, returns 201 Created with Location header
  • PUT /api/v1/beer/{beerId} — full update, returns 204 No Content
  • PATCH /api/v1/beer/{beerId} — partial update, returns 204 No Content
  • DELETE /api/v1/beer/{beerId} — deletes, returns 204 or throws NotFoundException

CustomerController.java (lines 1-76) — Same CRUD pattern at /api/v1/customer with:

  • GET /api/v1/customer — returns List<CustomerDTO>
  • GET /api/v1/customer/{customerId} — single customer
  • POST — creates, returns 201 + Location
  • PUT / PATCH / DELETE — same pattern as beer

@RequiredArgsConstructor (Lombok) generates a constructor for final fields, injecting the service.

Layer 3: Services (two implementations)

BeerServiceJPA.java — @Service @Primary (JPA-backed, used by default):

  • Uses BeerRepository + BeerMapper
  • listBeers() filters by name/style using Spring Data query methods:

- findAllByBeerNameIsLikeIgnoreCaseAndBeerStyle (both filters)
- findAllByBeerNameIsLikeIgnoreCase (name only)
- findAllByBeerStyle (style only)
- findAll() (no filters, ignores page params currently)

  • Optional pagination via buildPageRequest() (defaults: page 0, size 25, max 1000)
  • showInventory=false nullifies quantityOnHand in returned DTOs
  • Uses AtomicReference pattern for ifPresentOrElse updates
  • deleteById checks existsById() first, returns false if not found

BeerServiceImpl.java — @Service (non-primary, in-memory HashMap):

  • Seeds 3 hardcoded beers in constructor
  • Simple map-based CRUD, no persistence
  • listBeers() returns all beers regardless of filter params

CustomerServiceJPA.java / CustomerServiceImpl.java — Same dual pattern for customers, JPA version is @Primary.

Layer 4: Repositories (Spring Data JPA)

BeerRepository extends JpaRepository<Beer, UUID>:

  • findAllByBeerNameIsLikeIgnoreCase(String) — case-insensitive partial name match
  • findAllByBeerStyle(BeerStyle) — exact style match
  • findAllByBeerNameIsLikeIgnoreCaseAndBeerStyle(String, BeerStyle) — combined filter

CustomerRepository extends JpaRepository<Customer, UUID> — basic CRUD only.

Layer 5: Entities (JPA)

Beer (lines 1-64):

  • @Id @GeneratedValue(generator="UUID") @UuidGenerator — Hibernate UUID generation
  • @Version Integer version — optimistic locking
  • Fields: beerName (@Size 50), beerStyle (SMALLINT type code), upc, quantityOnHand, price (BigDecimal)
  • @CreationTimestamp / @UpdateTimestamp — auto-managed audit timestamps
  • Lombok: @Getter @Setter @Builder @NoArgsConstructor @AllArgsConstructor

Customer (lines 1-37):

  • UUID primary key, name, email (added in V2 migration), @Version, timestamps

Layer 6: DTOs (API contract)

BeerDTO (lines 1-53):

  • Fields: id (UUID), version (Integer), beerName (@NotBlank/@NotNull), beerStyle (@NotNull), upc (@NotBlank/@NotNull), quantityOnHand (Integer), price (@NotNull BigDecimal), createdDate, updateDate
  • @JsonDeserialize(builder=...) — Jackson configured for Lombok builder pattern

CustomerDTO (lines 1-29):

  • id, name, version, createdDate, updateDate

BeerStyle — enum: LAGER, PILSNER, STOUT, GOSE, PORTER, ALE, WHEAT, IPA, PALE_ALE, SAISON

Layer 7: Mappers (MapStruct)

BeerMapper / CustomerMapper — Simple @Mapper interfaces with two methods each:

  • beerDtoToBeer(BeerDTO) / beerToBeerDto(Beer)
  • customerDtoToCustomer(CustomerDTO) / customerToCustomerDto(Customer)

MapStruct generates implementations at compile time (configured in pom.xml with mapstruct.defaultComponentModel=spring).

Layer 8: Bootstrap Data

BootstrapData.java — @Component CommandLineRunner:

  1. loadBeerData() — seeds 3 beers if table empty
  2. loadCsvData() — if <10 beers exist, loads csvdata/beers.csv (~2426 records), maps CSV style strings to BeerStyle enum, sets price=10.00, saves all
  3. loadCustomerData() — seeds 3 customers

BeerCsvServiceImpl — Uses OpenCSV CsvToBeanBuilder to parse beers.csv into BeerCSVRecord beans.

Layer 9: Error Handling

NotFoundException — @ResponseStatus(NOT_FOUND) runtime exception, thrown when entity doesn't exist.

CustomErrorController — @ControllerAdvice:

  • handleJPAViolations() — catches TransactionSystemException → 400 Bad Request
  • handleBindErrors() — catches MethodArgumentNotValidException (validation failures) → 400 with list of field errors

Layer 10: Configuration

application.properties (default):

  • Debug logging, H2 in-memory DB, Flyway disabled, Docker compose disabled

application-localmysql.properties (localmysql profile):

  • MariaDB on 127.0.0.1:3306, DB restdb, user restadmin, Flyway enabled, ddl-auto=validate
  • HikariCP with optimized pool settings

Database Migrations (Flyway)

  • V1 — Creates beer and customer tables (InnoDB, UUID varchar PKs)
  • V2 — Adds email column to customer

Key Technical Choices

  1. Dual service implementations — BeerServiceImpl (in-memory) vs BeerServiceJPA (@Primary) — the JPA version is always used in production, in-memory likely for testing/fallback
  2. UUID primary keys via Hibernate's @UuidGenerator
  3. Optimistic locking with @Version
  4. MapStruct for compile-time mapping (faster than reflection-based mappers)
  5. OpenCSV for bulk data import
  6. Validation via @Valid, @NotBlank, @NotNull on DTO fields

260: ## Runtime workarounds used
261: 
262: - Local Maven executable: `/data/data/com.termux/files/usr/bin/mvn`.
263: - MariaDB was started directly because the Termux service wrapper expected an unavailable OS `mysql` user.
264: - Spring Boot Docker Compose auto-start was disabled because Docker is unavailable:
265: 
266:   ```bash
267:   mvn spring-boot:run \
268:     -Dspring-boot.run.profiles=localmysql \
269:     -Dspring-boot.run.jvmArguments='-Dspring.docker.compose.enabled=false'
270:   ```
271: 
272: - MariaDB 12.3 produced a Flyway warning because the installed Flyway version was verified through MariaDB 11.7.
273: - Hibernate emitted an `Unknown column 'RESERVED'` metadata warning against MariaDB, but schema validation and application startup completed successfully.
274: - The MariaDB server and Spring Boot process were stopped after each run, and ports 3306 and 8080 were verified as released.
275: 
276: ## Overall progression
277: 
278: The session progressed from Lombok model conveniences, through MySQL/MariaDB connectivity and pooling, to Flyway-owned schema migrations. It then added a customer column, introduced CSV parsing and database bootstrap persistence, automated entity timestamps, repaired integration-test expectations, and began layering beer-name query support from the controller down toward the repository.
279: 
280: ## Detailed implementation notes
281: 
282: ### MariaDB profile configuration
283: 
284: The active `localmysql` profile used throughout the runs contains the following important settings:
285: 
286: ```properties
287: spring.datasource.username=restadmin
288: spring.datasource.password=password
289: spring.datasource.url=jdbc:mysql://127.0.0.1:3306/restdb?useUnicode=true&characterEncoding=UTF-8&serverTimezone=UTC
290: spring.jpa.database=mysql
291: spring.datasource.driver-class-name=com.mysql.cj.jdbc.Driver
292: spring.jpa.properties.hibernate.dialect=org.hibernate.dialect.MySQLDialect
293: spring.jpa.hibernate.ddl-auto=validate
294: spring.flyway.enabled=true
295: ```
296: 
297: The profile deliberately points to `127.0.0.1` rather than a Docker hostname. That is why the application can run against the manually started MariaDB server in Termux. `ddl-auto=validate` means Hibernate checks the tables and columns but does not create or alter them; Flyway owns those changes.
298: 
299: The Hikari settings configure a five-connection pool named `RestDB-Pool`. Prepared-statement caching and server-prepared statements are enabled. SQL output, formatting, and JDBC bind-value tracing are also enabled, which explains the detailed Hibernate statements seen during POST and PATCH requests.
300: 
301: ### Flyway schema lifecycle
302: 
303: V1 creates two InnoDB tables. The beer table uses a `varchar(36)` UUID primary key, `beer_name varchar(50)`, `beer_style smallint`, decimal pricing, inventory quantity, UPC, timestamps, and an optimistic-locking `version` column. The customer table initially contains UUID, name, timestamps, and version.
304: 
305: V2 adds the customer email column without recreating the table. On branch 78, startup output showed:
306: 
307: ```text
308: Successfully validated 2 migrations
309: Current version of schema `restdb`: 1
310: Migrating schema `restdb` to version "2 - add-email-to-customer"
311: Successfully applied 1 migration
312: ```
313: 
314: On later branches, startup showed schema version 2 and `No migration necessary`, proving that Flyway history was reused rather than rerunning migrations.
315: 
316: ### UUID and MariaDB representation
317: 
318: The entities use `@UuidGenerator` for identifier generation. `@JdbcTypeCode(SqlTypes.CHAR)` tells Hibernate to bind UUID values as character data, matching the Flyway `varchar(36)` columns. Hibernate trace output confirmed bindings such as:
319: 
320: ```text
321: binding parameter (9:CHAR) <- [b98b91fc-1ec2-4b65-8a49-5deca3e1b594]
322: ```
323: 
324: This is the practical difference from the earlier failing branch: the Java UUID mapping and MariaDB column definition agree on a textual UUID representation rather than relying on an incompatible database-specific UUID mapping.
325: 
326: ### CSV mapping details
327: 
328: `BeerCSVRecord` represents the imported dataset. OpenCSV maps headers by name. The source data contains names such as `count.x`, `brewery_id`, and `count.y`; the explicit annotations prevent those names from being lost when mapped to Java fields.
329: 
330: The bootstrap conversion intentionally creates simplified application beers:
331: 
332: | CSV value | Stored beer value |
333: |---|---|
334: | `beer` | `beerName`, abbreviated to 50 characters |
335: | `style` | mapped to the application `BeerStyle` enum |
336: | `row` | `upc` as a string |
337: | `count.x` | `quantityOnHand` |
338: | no CSV price | fixed price `10` |
339: | CSV record | new generated UUID |
340: 
341: The style switch covers known dataset styles such as American IPA, American Pale Lager, American Porter, Oatmeal Stout, Saison/Farmhouse Ale, and English Pale Ale. Unknown styles fall back to `PILSNER`. This fallback prevents an unmapped source value from stopping the entire bootstrap operation, but it can also reduce semantic accuracy for styles not explicitly listed.
342: 
343: ### Bootstrap idempotence and test counts
344: 
345: `loadCsvData()` checks `beerRepository.count() < 10`. On a fresh schema, the three hand-written beers are inserted first, then the CSV rows are imported. On subsequent starts, the count is already above the threshold and the CSV is skipped. This prevents duplicate imports during ordinary restarts, but it is a coarse guard: it does not verify whether every expected CSV row exists.
346: 
347: The integration-test expected count changed from 3 to 2413 because the dataset adds approximately 2410 rows to the three initial beers. The repository test imports the service/bootstrap components into the JPA test context so the CSV data exists when testing the derived query.
348: 
349: ### Timestamp behavior
350: 
351: Before branch 84, timestamps were set manually in the initial bootstrap objects and could remain null for objects created through other paths. `@CreationTimestamp` and `@UpdateTimestamp` move timestamp assignment into Hibernate. A POST followed by GET on branch 84 returned values like:
352: 
353: ```json
354: {
355:   "createdDate": "2026-09-14T17:25:57.650782",
356:   "updateDate": "2026-09-14T17:25:57.651008",
357:   "version": 0
358: }
359: ```
360: 
361: The PATCH SQL updated `update_date` while leaving `created_date` unchanged. The entity version also incremented from 0 to 1, providing optimistic locking for updates.
362: 
363: ### Query progression and unfinished behavior
364: 
365: The query feature was intentionally developed in layers:
366: 
367: 1. Branch 86 added a test for a name query.
368: 2. Branch 87 added the optional HTTP request parameter.
369: 3. Branch 88 propagated the parameter through the service interface and implementations.
370: 4. Branch 89 added the Spring Data derived-query method and repository test.
371: 
372: The final branch inspected in the session still contained this unfinished method:
373: 
374: ```java
375: List<Beer> listBeersByName(String beerName) {
376:     return new ArrayList<>();
377: }
378: ```
379: 
380: Consequently, live `GET /api/v1/beer?beerName=IPA` did not yet return the expected 336 records. Depending on the active implementation path, the request returned the complete dataset or an empty name-filtered result. The repository method exists, but the service method must call it with wildcard values such as `%IPA%` before the feature is complete.
381: 
382: ### REST evidence collected
383: 
384: Across the runs, the stable REST behavior was:
385: 
386: ```text
387: GET /api/v1/customer       -> 200 with three customers
388: POST /api/v1/beer          -> 201 and a Location header containing a UUID
389: PATCH /api/v1/beer/{id}    -> 204 and an updated version/timestamp
390: POST invalid beer payload  -> 400 with validation messages
391: ```
392:

618: 
619: ### `93-query-complete-search-params`
620: 
621: Substantive commit: `01e6fe7 adding showInventory and complete search conditions`, merged into the branch line by `ad4181d Merge branch '92-query-beer-style-search' into 93-query-complete-search-params`.

624: 
625: #### Controller
626: 
627: `BeerController.listBeers` now accepts a third optional parameter and passes all three to the service:
628: 
629: ```java
630: public List<BeerDTO> listBeers(@RequestParam(required = false) String beerName,
631:                                @RequestParam(required = false) BeerStyle beerStyle,
632:                                @RequestParam(required = false) Boolean showInventory){
633:     return beerService.listBeers(beerName, beerStyle, showInventory);
634: }
635: ```
636: 
637: #### Service
638: 
639: `BeerService.listBeers` signature changed from two arguments to three across `BeerService`, `BeerServiceImpl`, and `BeerServiceJPA`. The in-memory `BeerServiceImpl` only follows the new signature; it still returns the full beer map without filtering.
640: 
641: `BeerServiceJPA.listBeers` now covers all four parameter combinations instead of falling back to `findAll()` when both filters are present:
642: 
643: | beerName | beerStyle | Repository call |
644: |---|---|---|
645: | set | null | `findAllByBeerNameIsLikeIgnoreCase("%" + beerName + "%")` |
646: | null | set | `findAllByBeerStyle(beerStyle)` |
647: | set | set | `findAllByBeerNameIsLikeIgnoreCaseAndBeerStyle("%" + beerName + "%", beerStyle)` |
648: | null | null | `findAll()` |
649: 
650: This resolves the branch-92 limitation where `?beerName=GUBNA&beerStyle=IPA` executed `findAll()` and returned all 2413 beers.
651: 
652: #### Repository
653: 
654: ```java
655: List<Beer> findAllByBeerNameIsLikeIgnoreCaseAndBeerStyle(String beerName, BeerStyle beerStyle);
656: ```
657: 
658: Spring Data derives a combined predicate from the method name: a case-insensitive name `LIKE` (upper-cased on both sides, matching the branch-91 generated SQL pattern `upper(beer_name) like upper(?)`) plus a style equality on the smallint-mapped `beer_style` column.
659: 
660: #### showInventory behavior
661: 
662: After the search branch runs, the service applies a response-shaping step:
663: 
664: ```java
665: if (showInventory != null && !showInventory) {
666:     beerList.forEach(beer -> beer.setQuantityOnHand(null));
667: }
668: ```
669: 
670: - `showInventory=true` or omitted (`null`) → `quantityOnHand` remains in the JSON response.
671: - `showInventory=false` → `quantityOnHand` is nulled on the loaded entity objects before DTO mapping, so the response omits the inventory value.
672: - The nulling operates on detached entities: the repository call's transaction has already ended and the service method is not transactional, so the change is never flushed to the database. It only affects the response payload.
673:

803: ## Branch 94: paging parameters
804: 
805: ### `94-page-add-parameters`
806: 
807: Branch created locally from `origin/94-page-add-parameters` (HEAD `85cdb07 Merge branch '93-query-complete-search-params' into 94-page-add-parameters`). This branch introduces paging as a new layer, following the same incremental pattern used for name and style search: parameters first, behavior later.
808: 
809: #### Changes
810: 
811: `BeerController.listBeers` accepts two more optional request parameters and passes all five to the service:
812: 
813: ```java
814: public List<BeerDTO> listBeers(@RequestParam(required = false) String beerName,
815:                                @RequestParam(required = false) BeerStyle beerStyle,
816:                                @RequestParam(required = false) Boolean showInventory,
817:                                @RequestParam(required = false) Integer pageNumber,
818:                                @RequestParam(required = false) Integer pageSize){
819:     return beerService.listBeers(beerName, beerStyle, showInventory, pageNumber, pageSize);
820: }
821: ```
822: 
823: The five-argument signature now flows through `BeerService`, `BeerServiceImpl`, and `BeerServiceJPA`.
824: 
825: #### Important: paging is not implemented yet
826: 
827: Only the signature changed. `BeerServiceJPA.listBeers` still runs the branch-93 search branches and returns every matching row — `pageNumber` and `pageSize` are accepted and immediately ignored:
828: 
829: ```java
830: public List<BeerDTO> listBeers(String beerName, BeerStyle beerStyle, Boolean showInventory,
831:                                Integer pageNumber, Integer pageSize) {
832:     ...
833:     beerList = listBeersByNameAndStyle(beerName, beerStyle);
834:     ...
835:     return beerList.stream().map(beerMapper::beerToBeerDto).collect(Collectors.toList());
836: }
837: ```
838: 
839: There is no `Pageable`, no `PageRequest`, and no slice on `beerList`. No repository method returns a `Page`/`Slice` either. So `?pageNumber=2&pageSize=50` currently returns the full result set, exactly as it would without those parameters.
840: 
841: #### Tests
842: 
843: - `BeerControllerIT` gained `tesListBeersByStyleAndNameShowInventoryTruePage2`, requesting `beerName=IPA&beerStyle=IPA&showInventory=true&pageNumber=2&pageSize=50` and expecting `$.size()` of 50 with a non-null first `quantityOnHand`.
844: - All existing controller and integration tests were mechanically updated to the five-argument signature, using `1, 25` as placeholder values.
845: - These placeholder values are never asserted; they exist only because the signature requires them, so the updates do not test paging behavior.
846: 
847: #### Verified failure
848: 
849: The new paging test was run to confirm the gap is real and not just a code-reading inference:
850: 
851: ```bash
852: mvn -o test -Dtest=BeerControllerIT#tesListBeersByStyleAndNameShowInventoryTruePage2
853: ```
854: 
855: ```text
856: [ERROR] BeerControllerIT.tesListBeersByStyleAndNameShowInventoryTruePage2:69 JSON path "$.size()"
857: Expected: is <50>
858:      but: was <310>
859: 
860: Tests run: 1, Failures: 1, Errors: 0, Skipped: 0
861: ```
862: 
863: The endpoint returned all 310 combined-matching records instead of the requested 50-row page, confirming the parameters are threaded but not applied. This is the expected state for this branch; the paging behavior is presumably the subject of the next branch.
864: 
865: #### Test environment note
866: 
867: This test ran against the H2/in-memory test datasource (the `@SpringBootTest` IT context bootstraps and seeds its own data), not the MariaDB `localmysql` profile. That is why the count is 310 — matching the branch-93 test expectation — rather than the live 325. MariaDB was not required and was not started for this run.
868: 
869: ## Branch 95: create page request
870: 
871: ### `95-page-create-page-request`
872: 
873: Branch created locally from `origin/95-page-create-page-request` (HEAD `b2f6b7b Merge branch '94-page-add-parameters' into 95-page-create-page-request`). Exactly one file changed: `BeerServiceJPA.java`, +31/−1.
874: 
875: #### Added constants
876: 
877: ```java
878: private static final int DEFAULT_PAGE = 0;
879: private static final int DEFAULT_PAGE_SIZE = 25;
880: ```
881: 
882: #### Added `buildPageRequest`
883: 
884: ```java
885: public PageRequest buildPageRequest(Integer pageNumber, Integer pageSize) {
886:     int queryPageNumber;
887:     int queryPageSize;
888: 
889:     if (pageNumber != null && pageNumber > 0) {
890:         queryPageNumber = pageNumber - 1;
891:     } else {
892:         queryPageNumber = DEFAULT_PAGE;
893:     }
894: 
895:     if (pageSize == null) {
896:         queryPageSize = DEFAULT_PAGE_SIZE;
897:     } else {
898:         if (pageSize > 1000) {
899:             queryPageSize = 1000;
900:         } else {
901:             queryPageSize = pageSize;
902:         }
903:     }
904: 
905:     return PageRequest.of(queryPageNumber, queryPageSize);
906: }
907: ```
908: 
909: Three policies are encoded here:
910: 
911: - **1-based to 0-based translation.** The public API is 1-based (`pageNumber=2` means the second page) while Spring Data is 0-based, so the method subtracts 1. A null, zero, or negative `pageNumber` falls back to `DEFAULT_PAGE` (0).
912: - **Page-size ceiling of 1000.** Any `pageSize` above 1000 is clamped, bounding how much a single request can load.
913: - **Defaults.** Omitted `pageNumber` yields page 0 and omitted `pageSize` yields 25.
914: 
915: An unguarded edge remains: `pageSize=0` or a negative value passes both checks and reaches `PageRequest.of(...)`, which requires a size of at least 1 and throws `IllegalArgumentException`. That would surface as a 500 rather than a clamped value or a 400.
916: 
917: #### Not yet applied
918: 
919: `listBeers` builds the request and then never uses it:
920: 
921: ```java
922: PageRequest pageRequest = buildPageRequest(pageNumber, pageSize);
923: 
924: List<Beer> beerList;
925: 
926: if(StringUtils.hasText(beerName) && beerStyle == null) {
927:     beerList = listBeersByName(beerName);
928: } else if ...
929: ```
930: 
931: The local variable is unused, and none of the repository methods take a `Pageable` or return `Page`/`Slice` — they still return `List<Beer>`. So this branch constructs the paging tool without connecting it to any query, and the endpoint's output is unchanged from branch 94.

READbranch-3-lombok-constructors.md
Read 1/1 file, 130 lines
1: # Branch 3 — `3-lombok-constructors`
2: 
3: This branch builds on branch `2-lombok-builders` and introduces a Spring MVC
4: `BeerController`, demonstrating Lombok's `@AllArgsConstructor` for
5: constructor-based dependency injection.
6: 
7: ## What changed from branch 2
8: 
9: Branch 2 ended with the `Beer` POJO and the `BeerService` / `BeerServiceImpl`
10: in place, but no controller. Branch 3 adds the controller layer and uses a
11: Lombok-generated constructor to wire in the `BeerService` dependency.
12: 
13: Single new file:
14: 
15: - `src/main/java/guru/springframework/spring7restmvc/controller/BeerController.java`
16: 
17: Full diff vs. `origin/2-lombok-builders`:
18: 
19: ```diff
20:  .../spring7restmvc/controller/BeerController.java   | 15 +++++++++++++++
21:  1 file changed, 15 insertions(+)
22: ```
23: 
24: ## The new code
25: 
26: `src/main/java/guru/springframework/spring7restmvc/controller/BeerController.java`:
27: 
28: ```java
29: package guru.springframework.spring7restmvc.controller;
30: 
31: import guru.springframework.spring7restmvc.services.BeerService;
32: import lombok.AllArgsConstructor;
33: import org.springframework.stereotype.Controller;
34: 
35: /**
36:  * Created by jt, Spring Framework Guru.
37:  */
38: @AllArgsConstructor
39: @Controller
40: public class BeerController {
41:     private final BeerService beerService;
42: 
43: }
44: ```
45: 
46: ## Line-by-line explanation
47: 
48: - `package guru.springframework.spring7restmvc.controller;`
49:   Namespace for the web/controller layer of the application.
50: 
51: - `import guru.springframework.spring7restmvc.services.BeerService;`
52:   Brings in the service abstraction defined in branch 1/2.
53: 
54: - `import lombok.AllArgsConstructor;`
55:   Lombok annotation which, at compile time, generates a constructor
56:   accepting every field in the class as a parameter.
57: 
58: - `import org.springframework.stereotype.Controller;`
59:   Marks the class as a Spring MVC controller so it is registered as a
60:   bean during component scanning and can handle web requests.
61: 
62: - `@AllArgsConstructor`
63:   Instructs Lombok to generate:
64:   ```java
65:   public BeerController(BeerService beerService) {
66:       this.beerService = beerService;
67:   }
68:   ```
69:   This is the classic (preferred) way to do constructor-based dependency
70:   injection in Spring. The generated constructor makes the dependency
71:   explicit, the field can be `final`, and the class is easy to unit-test.
72: 
73: - `@Controller`
74:   Stereotype annotation that registers the class as a Spring-managed
75:   component. In Spring MVC, `@Controller` (combined with handler-method
76:   annotations like `@GetMapping`) is what makes a class capable of
77:   serving HTTP requests. In Spring WebFlux the equivalent would be
78:   `@RestController` for REST endpoints; here the class is a stub that
79:   will grow into a `@RestController` in later branches.
80: 
81: - `public class BeerController { ... }`
82:   The controller class. At this stage it is intentionally empty of
83:   behavior — the goal of the branch is purely to demonstrate the
84:   Lombok-generated constructor pattern.
85: 
86: - `private final BeerService beerService;`
87:   The single dependency. It is `final` because it is supplied via the
88:   constructor and never reassigned. `final` fields are a strong signal
89:   that the dependency is required and immutable for the lifetime of
90:   the bean.
91: 
92: - Empty body `{}`
93:   No request-handling methods yet. Later branches (e.g.
94:   `5-list-beer`, `6-get-by-id`, `9-http-post`) will add handler methods
95:   that call `beerService` to serve REST endpoints.
96: 
97: ## Why `@AllArgsConstructor` here
98: 
99: - Avoids boilerplate: no need to hand-write the constructor.
100: - Promotes constructor injection over field injection — easier to test,
101:   fields can be `final`, and required dependencies are obvious.
102: - Keeps the controller minimal; subsequent branches only need to add
103:   handler methods, not plumbing.
104: 
105: ## Prerequisites carried over from previous branches
106: 
107: For this branch to compile, the following must already be in place
108: (from branches 1 and 2):
109: 
110: - Lombok on the classpath/annotation processor path in `pom.xml`
111:   (Lombok is already configured for Spring Boot 4 / Java 25 in `pom.xml`).
112: - `BeerService` interface with a `getBeerById(UUID)` method.
113: - `BeerServiceImpl` returning a `Beer` built via Lombok's `@Builder`.
114: - `Beer` POJO annotated with `@Builder` and `@Data`.
115: 
116: ## Build / verify
117: 
118: ```bash
119: ./mvnw clean compile
120: ```
121: 
122: After compilation, `BeerController` will have a synthetic constructor
123: `BeerController(BeerService beerService)`, and Spring will autowire
124: the `BeerServiceImpl` bean into it when the application context starts.
125: 
126: ## Next branch
127: 
128: Branch `4-lombok-logging` adds Lombok's `@Slf4j` (or equivalent) to give
129: the controller (and other classes) a logger field without manual
130: declaration.

Thinking
This branch 96 `refactor-spring-data-methods` implements the paging feature that was stubbed in branch 95. The key changes are:

1. **BeerController.java** - Changed return type from `List<BeerDTO>` to `Page<BeerDTO>` for the `listBeers` method, added `import org.springframework.data.domain.Page`

2. **BeerRepository.java** - Changed all three query methods from returning `List<Beer>` to `Page<Beer>` and added `Pageable` parameter:
   - `findAllByBeerNameIsLikeIgnoreCase(String beerName, Pageable pageable)` → `Page<Beer>`
   - `findAllByBeerStyle(BeerStyle beerStyle, Pageable pageable)` → `Page<Beer>`
   - `findAllByBeerNameIsLikeIgnoreCaseAndBeerStyle(String beerName, BeerStyle beerStyle, Pageable pageable)` → `Page<Beer>`

3. **BeerService.java** - Changed interface return type from `List<BeerDTO>` to `Page<BeerDTO>`

4. **BeerServiceImpl.java** (in-memory) - Changed return type to `Page<BeerDTO>` and wrapped in `PageImpl`

5. **BeerServiceJPA.java** - Major changes:
   - Changed all `listBeers*` helper methods to accept `Pageable` and return `Page<Beer>` instead of `List<Beer>`
   - Uses `PageRequest` from `buildPageRequest()` to pass paging info to repository methods
   - Uses `PageRequest` for `findAll()` too
   - Returns `beerPage.map(beerMapper::beerToBeerDto)` instead of stream/collect
   - Removed `Collectors` import, added `Page` and `Pageable` imports

6. **Tests** - Updated to use `Page` API:
   - `BeerControllerIT` - jsonPath `$.size()` → `$.content.size()`, `$.[0]` → `$.content[0]`
   - `BeerControllerTest` - `.get(0)` → `.getContent().get(0)`
   - `BeerRepositoryTest` - `List<Beer>` → `Page<Beer>`, `.size()` → `.getContent().size()`, added `null` for Pageable param (uses default)
   - Added `pageSize=800` query params to IT tests to get full result sets (since default page size is 25 and max is 1000)
   - `testListBeers` expects 1000 (clamped max) instead of 2413 when requesting page size 2413

Now let me run the tests to verify.

Branch 96 implements actual paging support. The key changes:

  1. Repository methods now accept Pageable and return Page<Beer> instead of List<Beer>
  2. BeerServiceJPA passes the buildPageRequest() (built in branch 95) to all repository calls
  3. Controller/Service return types changed from List<BeerDTO> to Page<BeerDTO>
  4. PageRequest is finally connected to queries — paging actually works now

Snippet (lines 273-312 of 1021):
273	- Hibernate emitted an `Unknown column 'RESERVED'` metadata warning against MariaDB, but schema validation and application startup completed successfully.
274	- The MariaDB server and Spring Boot process were stopped after each run, and ports 3306 and 8080 were verified as released.
275	
276	## Branch 96: refactor Spring Data methods for paging
277	
278	### `96-refactor-spring-data-methods`
279	
280	Branch created from `origin/96-refactor-spring-data-methods`. This branch completes the paging feature that branch 95 stubbed: the `PageRequest` created in `buildPageRequest()` is now threaded through the repository layer to actually limit results.
281	
282	#### Changes
283	
284	**Repository layer (`BeerRepository.java`)** — All three query methods changed from `List<Beer>` to `Page<Beer>` and accept a `Pageable` parameter:
285	
286	```java
287	Page<Beer> findAllByBeerNameIsLikeIgnoreCase(String beerName, Pageable pageable);
288	Page<Beer> findAllByBeerStyle(BeerStyle beerStyle, Pageable pageable);
289	Page<Beer> findAllByBeerNameIsLikeIgnoreCaseAndBeerStyle(String beerName, BeerStyle beerStyle, Pageable pageable);
290	```
291	
292	**Service interface (`BeerService.java`)** — Return type changed from `List<BeerDTO>` to `Page<BeerDTO>`.
293	
294	**JPA service (`BeerServiceJPA.java`)** — Key changes:
295	- `listBeers()` passes the `PageRequest` (built from `buildPageRequest()`) to all repository methods instead of the earlier `List`-returning stubs.
296	- Helper methods `listBeersByName`, `listBeersByStyle`, `listBeersByNameAndStyle` now accept and forward `Pageable`.
297	- `findAll()` (the no-filter case) now calls `beerRepository.findAll(pageRequest)` instead of `beerRepository.findAll()`.
298	- The return is now `beerPage.map(beerMapper::beerToBeerDto)` — a single `Page.map()` call instead of `stream().map().collect()`. This preserves the `Page` metadata (total elements, total pages, etc.).
299	- Removed `import java.util.List` and `java.util.stream.Collectors` in favor of `Page` / `Pageable` imports.
300	
301	**In-memory service (`BeerServiceImpl.java`)** — `listBeers()` now returns `PageImpl<>(new ArrayList<>(beerMap.values()))` to satisfy the new `Page<BeerDTO>` contract. This is a thin wrapper — the in-memory impl does not filter or paginate; it just wraps the full list in a `Page`.
302	
303	**Controller (`BeerController.java`)** — `listBeers()` return type changed from `List<BeerDTO>` to `Page<BeerDTO>`, added `import org.springframework.data.domain.Page`. The `@RequestParam` parameters (`pageNumber`, `pageSize`) are unchanged from branch 95; they just now have an effect.
304	
305	#### JSON response shape
306	
307	Because the controller now returns Spring Data `Page<T>` directly, the JSON response structure changed:
308	
309	**Before (List):**
310	```json
311	[{"beerName": "...", ...}, {"beerName": "...", ...}]
312	```

323:   "numberOfElements": 25,
324:   ...
325: }
326: ```
327: 
328: This means clients must now look at `$.content` for the actual beer array rather than `$.` directly. Spring logged a warning: *"Serializing PageImpl instances as-is is not supported... use Spring Data's PagedModel"* — this is a known limitation of returning `Page` directly from a REST controller without `PageableHandlerInterceptor` / `PagedResourcesAssembler`.
329:

335: - **`BeerControllerTest`** — Changed all `beerServiceImpl.listBeers(...)` return handling from `.get(0)` to `.getContent().get(0)` since the return is now a `Page`. Mock returns updated to `Page`.

46:         } else if (StringUtils.hasText(beerName) && beerStyle != null){

55:

58:     }

61:         int queryPageNumber;
62:         int queryPageSize;

64:         if (pageNumber != null && pageNumber > 0) {

Branch 97 — 97-paging-add-sort

This branch is a small, focused change: +4/−1 lines in one file (BeerServiceJPA.java). It adds ascending sort-by-name to every paginated beer query.

What changed:

  1. Added import org.springframework.data.domain.Sort.
  1. In buildPageRequest(), it now constructs a sort and passes it to PageRequest.of(...):
Sort sort = Sort.by(Sort.Order.asc("beerName"));

return PageRequest.of(queryPageNumber, queryPageSize, sort);

Why it matters:

  • Before this branch, PageRequest.of(page, size) had no ordering. Since SQL LIMIT/OFFSET without ORDER BY has no guaranteed order, paging could return rows in arbitrary (and potentially inconsistent) order across requests — the same page could show different rows between calls. Adding a deterministic sort fixes that.
  • Sort.by(Sort.Order.asc("beerName")) sorts by the entity's beerName property (JPA translates it to the beer_name column). Sort.Order.asc(...) sets the direction; Sort.by(...) takes a varargs list of orders, so multiple sort keys can be chained later (Sort.by(asc("beerName"), desc("price"))).
  • Because the sort is built inside buildPageRequest(), all four query paths (name, style, name+style, and findAll) inherit the ordering automatically — no repository or controller change was needed.

Verified at runtime:

Hibernate: ... order by b1_0.beer_name limit ?, ?
  • GET /api/v1/beer?pageNumber=1&pageSize=5 starts at #001 Golden Amber Lager, #002 American I.P.A., ...
  • GET /api/v1/beer?pageNumber=2&pageSize=5 continues at #9, 077XX, 10 Degrees of Separation, ... — a clean alphabetical progression.
  • Response metadata: totalElements: 2429, totalPages: 486, size: 5, sort.sorted: true.

The app is running on port 8080 against MariaDB (localmysql profile), so you can curl it directly:

curl "http://localhost:8080/api/v1/beer?pageNumber=2&pageSize=5"

Want me to also update SESSION_BRANCH_CHANGELOG.md with the branch-97 entry, or stop the app and MariaDB?

355	#### Change
356	
357	Added `import org.springframework.data.domain.Sort;` and a sort clause inside `buildPageRequest()`:

365	#### Why
366	
367	- Branch 96 wired `PageRequest` into the queries, but `PageRequest.of(page, size)` carries no ordering. SQL `LIMIT/OFFSET` without `ORDER BY` has no guaranteed row order, so paged results could be arbitrary and even inconsistent between requests — the same page number could show different rows on separate calls.
368	- `Sort.by(Sort.Order.asc("beerName"))` sorts by the entity's `beerName` property, which JPA maps to the `beer_name` column. `Sort.Order.asc(...)` sets direction; `Sort.by(...)` accepts multiple orders, so additional keys can be chained later (e.g. `Sort.by(asc("beerName"), desc("price"))`).
369	- Because the sort lives in `buildPageRequest()`, all four query paths — name, style, name+style, and the no-filter `findAll` — inherit it with no controller or repository change.

Checked out 98-rel-add-order-order-detail, ran the app, and updated the changelog.

The branch: schema-only groundwork — no Java changes. It adds one manual DDL script, src/scripts/add-order-tables.sql, creating two tables:

  • beer_order (header) with customer_id FK → customer.id
  • beer_order_line (details) with FKs → beer_order.id and beer.id

This is the one-to-many order/order-line backbone that later branches will map as JPA entities.

Key observations:

  • It's not a Flyway migration — it lives in src/scripts/ (not db/migration/) with no V*__ prefix, so Flyway never runs it. It's applied by hand.
  • drop table if exists is ordered correctly (line before order) to respect the FK dependency.
  • version bigint here, while beer/customer use integer — and last_modified_date differs from the existing update_date, so entity mappings will need explicit @Column names later.

Verified at runtime:

  • Applied the script to restdb; all three FKs confirmed via information_schema.
  • App started clean on the localmysql profile — Flyway reported No migration necessary, Hibernate validate didn't object to the extra tables, and Spring Data still reports 2 JPA repositories (no Order entities yet).

493	
494	This branch is schema-only groundwork: the tables and foreign keys exist so that the next branches can add the JPA entities, repositories, and mappings that use them.
495	
496	## Branch 99: order tables as a Flyway migration
497	
498	### `99-rel-add-order-order-detail`
499	
500	Branch created from `origin/99-rel-add-order-order-detail`. Again **no Java changes**. This branch takes the DDL script that branch 98 added by hand and moves it into Flyway's managed migration path as `src/main/resources/db/migration/V3__add-order-tables.sql` (29 lines, byte-identical content to branch 98's `src/scripts/add-order-tables.sql`).
501	
502	#### What changed
503	
504	- Added `V3__add-order-tables.sql` under `src/main/resources/db/migration/`, so Flyway discovers, validates, and applies it on startup.
505	- The branch-98 manual script `src/scripts/add-order-tables.sql` is **still tracked** — both files coexist. The `src/scripts/` copy is now redundant reference material; the Flyway copy is the one that actually shapes the database.
506	
507	#### Why this matters
508	
509	Branch 98's script required someone to run it manually (`mysql < src/scripts/add-order-tables.sql`). Branch 99 makes the schema change automatic and versioned:
510	
511	- **Automatic on startup.** Application boot now creates the order tables without any manual step.
512	- **Recorded and checksummed.** Flyway writes a row to `flyway_schema_history` and hashes the file, so later edits to `V3` would be detected as a checksum mismatch.
513	- **Ordered after V1/V2.** The `V3` prefix guarantees the order tables are created only after `customer` and `beer` exist, satisfying the foreign keys.
514	
515	#### The running-state detail worth noting
516	
517	In the branch-98 step of this session, the order tables had already been created by hand — so before branch 99's app start, `beer_order` / `beer_order_line` physically existed but were **absent from `flyway_schema_history`** (history was at version 2). Flyway therefore treated V3 as unapplied and ran it. Because V3 begins with:
518	
519	```sql
520	drop table if exists beer_order_line;
521	drop table if exists beer_order;
522	```
523	
524	it dropped the manually created tables and recreated them under Flyway's ownership. This is why the migration is safe to run against a database where the tables already exist, but also why embedding `drop table` in a versioned migration is unusual for production use — it is destructive if any data were present. Here the tables were empty, so nothing was lost.
525	
526	#### Empirical evidence
527	
528	Startup with the `localmysql` profile:
529	
530	```text

+import jakarta.persistence.*;
+import lombok.*;
+import org.hibernate.annotations.*;
+import org.hibernate.type.SqlTypes;
+
+import java.sql.Timestamp;
+import java.util.UUID;
+
+@Getter
+@Setter
+@Entity
+@NoArgsConstructor
+@AllArgsConstructor
+@Builder
+public class BeerOrder {
+
+    @Id
+    @GeneratedValue(generator = "UUID")
+    @UuidGenerator
+    @JdbcTypeCode(SqlTypes.CHAR)
+    @Column(length = 36, columnDefinition = "varchar(36)", updatable = false, nullable = false )
+    private UUID id;
+
+    @Version
+    private Long version;
+
+    @CreationTimestamp
+    @Column(updatable = false)
+    private Timestamp createdDate;
+
+    @UpdateTimestamp
+    private Timestamp lastModifiedDate;
+
+    public boolean isNew() {
+        return this.id == null;
+    }
+
+    private String customerRef;
+
+    @ManyToOne
+    private Customer customer;
+
+}

| Association | Owning | Inverse | Join column |
|---|---|---|---|
| Customer ↔ BeerOrder | BeerOrder.customer | Customer.beerOrders | customer_id |
| BeerOrder ↔ BeerOrderLine | BeerOrderLine.beerOrder | BeerOrder.beerOrderLines | beer_order_id |
| Beer ↔ BeerOrderLine | BeerOrderLine.beer | Beer.beerOrderLines | beer_id |

Design notes: default fetch types (eager @ManyToOne, lazy @OneToMany) mean the two eager associations on BeerOrderLine can produce N+1 queries; there's still no cascade/orphanRemoval, so order lines won't persist automatically; and the collections are uninitialized. Let me write.

Here's the logic behind what branch 102 adds, layer by layer.

The repository — what actually happens

public interface BeerOrderRepository extends JpaRepository<BeerOrder, UUID> {}

There's no implementation class, yet the bean works. The logic: at startup Spring Data JPA scans for interfaces extending Repository, and for each one generates a dynamic proxy (SimpleJpaRepository-backed) registered in the container. JpaRepository<BeerOrder, UUID> tells it two things: the managed type is BeerOrder and the primary key type is UUID. From that it derives the table (beer_order), the id column (id), and gives you save, findById, findAll, count, delete, plus Page/Sort overloads — all without you writing SQL. Derived-query methods (like findAllByBeerNameIsLikeIgnoreCase) would work too, but none are declared here.

No BeerOrderLineRepository exists on purpose: lines are owned by the order aggregate. You reach them through BeerOrder.beerOrderLines, not through a separate root.

The test — the logic of the setup and why it's weak

@BeforeEach
void setUp() {
    testCustomer = customerRepository.findAll().get(0);
    testBeer = beerRepository.findAll().get(0);
}

@BeforeEach runs before each test method, re-fetching fresh instances. .get(0) assumes the repositories are non-empty — that's guaranteed here because the context is a full @SpringBootTest, so BootstrapData (a CommandLineRunner) has already run and seeded 3 customers and the CSV beers. On a repository-level @DataJpaTest this line would blow up with IndexOutOfBoundsException, since the slice wouldn't run the bootstrap. That's the key logic difference: @SpringBootTest boots the whole app (bootstrap included), @DataJpaTest boots only the JPA slice (no bootstrap).

@Test
void testBeerOrders() {
    System.out.println(beerOrderRepository.count());
    ...
}

The logic is purely demonstrative. count() issues select count(*) from beer_order — returning 0 because nothing has ever been persisted to that table. The other prints confirm the other two repositories resolve in the same context. There are no assertions, so the method encodes no expected behavior: it passes as long as nothing throws. Its only real value is proving the three beans wire together and can hit the database.

The persistence logic underneath (why count is 0)

The order model from branches 100–101 is bidirectional with the owning side on the @ManyToOnes:

  • BeerOrder.customer owns customer_id
  • BeerOrderLine.beerOrder owns beer_order_id
  • BeerOrderLine.beer owns beer_id

Because the inverse sides use mappedBy, JPA reads the join columns from the owning side only. And critically there's no cascade anywhere — so saving a BeerOrder does not persist its beerOrderLines, and saving a BeerOrderLine does not require the order to exist first (the FK columns are nullable). That's why the aggregate is still empty: no service code has called save(...) on the repository yet.

Where this sits in the flow

Controller → Service → BeerOrderRepository → BeerOrder (+ BeerOrderLine, Customer, Beer)

Branch 102 supplies only the third box. With no service/DTO/mapper/controller, the logic chain terminates at the repository — which is exactly why the HTTP API is unchanged and why the test can only count() rather than create and assert an order.

Want me to walk through the likely service-layer logic (persist order + lines, cascade or manual, DTO mapping) that the next branches add?

The next branches are expected to build the order service and API on top of this repository.
918	
919	## Branch 103: persisting relationships
920	
921	### `103-rel-persisting-relationships`
922	
923	Branch created from `origin/103-rel-persisting-relationships`. **Only one file changed** — `BeerOrderRepositoryTest.java`, +24/−6. No production code. The branch converts the branch-102 print-only test into one that actually **persists** a `BeerOrder` that references a `Customer`.
924	
925	#### The new test
926	
927	```java
928	@Transactional
929	@Test
930	void testBeerOrders() {
931	    BeerOrder beerOrder = BeerOrder.builder()
932	            .customerRef("Test order")
933	            .customer(testCustomer)
934	            .build();
935	
936	    BeerOrder savedBeerOrder = beerOrderRepository.saveAndFlush(beerOrder);
937	
938	    System.out.println(savedBeerOrder.getCustomerRef());
939	}
940	```
941	
942	#### The logic, line by line
943	
944	- **`@Transactional` on the test method** is the crucial addition. Spring's test framework wraps a `@Transactional` test in a transaction and **rolls it back automatically** when the method ends. So the insert is real and observed by the code inside the test, but it never survives the test. This is why the branch can persist orders without polluting the database and without needing cleanup code.
945	- **`.customer(testCustomer)`** wires the already-loaded (first) customer into the order's `@ManyToOne`. This exercises the owning side of the Customer↔BeerOrder association — the value written to `beer_order.customer_id`.
946	- **`.customerRef("Test order")`** sets the plain denormalized string column.
947	- **`saveAndFlush(...)`** (rather than `save`) forces the SQL INSERT to execute immediately and returns the managed instance. That matters because the entity id is generated by Hibernate (`@UuidGenerator`), the `@Version` is assigned, and `@CreationTimestamp`/`@UpdateTimestamp` are populated — `saveAndFlush` guarantees those are realized before the next line runs. With plain `save` the insert might be deferred until flush/commit, and the returned object's generated fields could be unset at the point of use.
948	- **`savedBeerOrder.getCustomerRef()`** prints the persisted value, confirming the entity round-tripped through the repository.
949	
950	#### What this demonstrates about relationship persistence
951	
952	The order holds a **reference to an existing** customer, so no cascade is involved: Hibernate only needs the customer's id to write the FK. The detached `testCustomer` loaded in `@BeforeEach` (its own transaction) is sufficient — assigning a detached entity to an owning `@ManyToOne` is fine when the row already exists. This is the pattern a future order service will use: look up the customer, attach it to the order, save the order. Nothing about `BeerOrderLine` is exercised yet, so the "save an order *with lines*" case (which needs cascade or explicit line saves) is still ahead.

@Transactional
@Test

The logic:

  • saveAndFlush (not save) forces the INSERT immediately so the generated id, @Version, and timestamps are realized before the next line.
  • Still no assertions — it's a demonstration that the mapping can carry out a relationship insert.

Branch 104 adds helper methods for maintaining both sides of the relationship:

1. **BeerOrder**:
   - `@AllArgsConstructor` removed, replaced with an explicit constructor that calls `this.setCustomer(customer)` instead of direct assignment
   - New `setCustomer(Customer)` method (overrides Lombok's setter) that sets the field AND adds `this` to `customer.getBeerOrders()`
   
2. **Customer**:
   - `beerOrders` initialized to `new HashSet<>()` with `@Builder.Default`

3. **Test**: changed `saveAndFlush` back to `save`

Key logic: This is the classic "synchronize both sides of a bidirectional relationship" helper. The `setCustomer` helper keeps the inverse collection in sync.

Important issues to note:
- **Potential NPE / recursion**: `setCustomer` calls `customer.getBeerOrders().add(this)`. If `customer` is null → NPE. Also, `Customer.beerOrders` is now initialized, so no NPE there.
- **The explicit constructor is redundant with Lombok's builder** — actually it's needed because `@AllArgsConstructor` was removed; the builder uses it? No, Lombok's `@Builder` on the class generates a builder using all fields via its own constructor. Actually `@Builder` on a class requires either an all-args constructor or... Lombok `@Builder` generates an all-args package-private constructor if none exists. Since an explicit constructor exists now, Lombok's @Builder will use... hmm. Actually `@Builder` placed on the class generates a builder that calls the all-args constructor. If you define your own constructor, Lombok's @Builder needs an all-args constructor to build from; if it can't find one, it generates one. But here a manual all-args-like constructor exists (with the call to setCustomer). Lombok `@Builder` on type will use the explicit all-args constructor if present? 

Let me think carefully. Lombok `@Builder` on a class: it generates a builder that uses a constructor with all fields. If `@AllArgsConstructor` is present, it uses that. If no constructor exists, Lombok generates an all-args constructor. If a user-defined constructor exists (matching all fields), Lombok's @Builder will use it if it's the "all args" constructor. Actually, Lombok's @Builder requires an all-args constructor; if there's exactly one constructor and it has all fields, it uses it. Here the manual constructor has all 7 fields in the same order, so @Builder uses it. That's the trick — so that building via builder calls setCustomer and keeps both sides in sync.

Wait, but the manual constructor signature order: (UUID id, Long version, Timestamp createdDate, Timestamp lastModifiedDate, String customerRef, Customer customer, Set<BeerOrderLine> beerOrderLines). The field order in the class: id, version, createdDate, lastModifiedDate, customerRef, customer, beerOrderLines. Matches. So @Builder picks it up.

Hmm, but actually Lombok is picky: `@Builder` on a class uses the all-args constructor only if it exists, else generates. With an explicit constructor present, Lombok will treat it as the all-args constructor if it has the same number/types as the fields. So yes.

So the logic: builder.build() → constructor → setCustomer → customer.getBeerOrders().add(this). This keeps the inverse set in sync at construction time.

- **The test still uses `.customer(testCustomer)`** — with the builder, `.customer(...)` calls the builder's customer setter, which sets the field directly (not the entity's setCustomer). Then `build()` invokes the constructor, which calls `this.setCustomer(customer)`. So the sync happens. Good.

- `setCustomer` has no null check → NPE if customer is null. Calling `beerOrder.setCustomer(null)` would NPE. Also, in the constructor, if customer is null (e.g., building an order without a customer), it NPEs. This is a real fragility.

Let me summarize.

Checked out 104-rel-helper-methods, ran the app and test, and updated the changelog. (MariaDB was already up, so I reused it rather than restarting — per your preference.)

The branch: adds the classic bidirectional-consistency helper pattern (3 files, +20/−3):

  • BeerOrder — drops @AllArgsConstructor and hand-writes an equivalent constructor whose only difference is routing the customer through this.setCustomer(customer). It also adds setCustomer(...), which assigns the owning field and adds this to customer.getBeerOrders().
  • Customer — initializes beerOrders = new HashSet<>() with @Builder.Default.
  • Test — saveAndFlush reverted to save.

The trick: Lombok's @Builder constructs objects through the all-args constructor, so removing @AllArgsConstructor and adding a manual one means builder.build() now automatically routes through the syncing setter — both sides stay consistent for free. @Builder.Default on the HashSet is required because otherwise Lombok's builder would leave the collection null, and the helper's .add(this) would NPE.

Caveats worth flagging:

  • setCustomer has no null guard — building an order without a customer now NPEs, since the builder always calls the constructor.
  • Sync is one-directional only — no Customer.addBeerOrder, no removeBeerOrder, and no equivalent helper for BeerOrderLine.

Verified: app booted clean (3 repositories, No migration necessary, 2429 beers), and the test passes (Tests run: 1, Failures: 0, Errors: 0), exercising the builder→constructor→setCustomer path. Still no orders persisted and no order REST API yet.

App and MariaDB are running. Stop, or continue to branch 105?

+package guru.springframework.spring7restmvc.entities;
+
+import jakarta.persistence.*;
+import lombok.AllArgsConstructor;
+import lombok.Getter;
+import lombok.NoArgsConstructor;
+import lombok.Setter;
+import org.hibernate.annotations.CreationTimestamp;
+import org.hibernate.annotations.GenericGenerator;
+import org.hibernate.annotations.JdbcTypeCode;
+import org.hibernate.annotations.UpdateTimestamp;
+import org.hibernate.type.SqlTypes;
+
+import java.sql.Timestamp;
+import java.util.Set;
+import java.util.UUID;
+
+@Getter
+@Setter
+@NoArgsConstructor
+@AllArgsConstructor
+@Entity
+public class Category {
+
+    @Id
+    @GeneratedValue(generator = "UUID")
+    @GenericGenerator(name = "UUID", strategy = "org.hibernate.id.UUIDGenerator")
+    @JdbcTypeCode(SqlTypes.CHAR)
+    @Column(length = 36, columnDefinition = "varchar(36)", updatable = false, nullable = false )
+    private UUID id;
+
+    @Version
+    private Long version;
+
+    @CreationTimestamp
+    @Column(updatable = false)
+    private Timestamp createdDate;
+
+    @UpdateTimestamp
+    private Timestamp lastModifiedDate;
+
+    private String description;
+
+    @ManyToMany
+    @JoinTable(name = "beer_category",
+      joinColumns = @JoinColumn(name = "category_id"),
+      inverseJoinColumns = @JoinColumn(name = "beer_id"))
+    private Set<Beer> beers;
+
+
+
+    @Override
+    public boolean equals(Object o) {
+        if (this == o) return true;
+        if (!(o instanceof Category)) return false;
+        if (!super.equals(o)) return false;
+
+        Category category = (Category) o;
+
+        return getDescription() != null ? getDescription().equals(category.getDescription()) : category.getDescription() == null;
+    }
+
+    @Override
+    public int hashCode() {
+        int result = super.hashCode();
+        result = 31 * result + (getDescription() != null ? getDescription().hashCode() : 0);
+        return result;
+    }
+}

Typically you'd have one side with `mappedBy`. Here both sides map the join table explicitly (which is technically allowed but redundant/disallowed? Actually Hibernate allows it but it can cause issues). Hmm.

Wait — in JPA, for a bidirectional M2M, one side is the owner (@JoinTable) and the other uses mappedBy. If both sides declare @JoinTable with the same table name and swapped columns, Hibernate may complain about duplicate table or may work but both sides are owners. Actually Hibernate does allow it and treats it as two separate associations pointing at the same table — it can work but is generally discouraged.

Let me summarize.

Checked out 105-many-to-many, ran the app, and updated the changelog. (MariaDB was already up, so I reused it.)

The branch: adds a many-to-many Beer↔Category (3 files, +96):

  • Category (new entity) — UUID id, Long version, timestamps, description, a @ManyToMany Set<Beer> beers, and equals/hashCode overridden on description.
  • Beer — gains @ManyToMany @JoinTable(name="beer_category", joinColumns=beer_id, inverseJoinColumns=category_id) Set<Category> categories.
  • V4__category.sql — creates category and the join table beer_category (composite PK (beer_id, category_id) + two FKs).

Notable design points:

  • It's a *bidirectional M2M where both sides declare @JoinTable* on the same table with mirrored columns, instead of one side using mappedBy. Hibernate accepts it and validation passed, but there's no single owning side — a config the next branch likely revisits for persistence.
  • @GenericGenerator is used here instead of @UuidGenerator — it compiles but Hibernate emits has been deprecated and marked for removal warnings.
  • equals/hashCode on a mutable description is a Set/Map hazard, and makes two same-description categories "equal" despite different ids.
  • V4's drop order is reversed vs V3 — it drops category (the referenced parent) before beer_category. Harmless on a fresh DB (both absent, no-ops) and Flyway runs it once, but it would fail if re-run against a populated schema.
  • Minor: created_date timestamp vs last_modified_date datetime(6) in one table; and varchar(50) vs Hibernate's default 255 length (validation doesn't check lengths).

Verified: Flyway applied V4 (now at version v4), history 1–4 all successful, beer_category composite PK + FKs confirmed, app healthy (2429 beers), still 3 repositories (no CategoryRepository).
